Uttama Carita · Chapter 8 of 13 · 63 mantras (8.1–8.63)

रक्तबीजवधः

raktabījavadhaḥ

The Slaying of Raktabīja · Complete with Noted Variants

Introduction

Summary
Medhas takes up the story (8.1). With Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa slain and their many troops destroyed (8.2), Śumbha, his mind mastered by anger, orders the whole Daitya host to march (8.3): eighty-six clans with weapons raised and eighty-four clans of the Kambus (8.4), fifty Koṭivīrya clans and a hundred of the Dhaumras (8.5), the Kālakas, Daurhṛdas, Mauryas and Kālakeyas (8.6); and for the first time he sets out himself, with many thousands of troops (8.7). Seeing him come, Caṇḍikā fills the space between earth and sky with the twang of her bowstring (8.8); the lion roars, and Ambikā swells the roar with her bell (8.9); Kālī, her mouth gaping, outdoes all three sounds with her own (8.10). The Daitya armies close in on the Goddess, the lion and Kālī (8.11). At that moment, to destroy the gods’ enemies, the Śaktis of Brahmā, Śiva, Skanda, Viṣṇu and Indra leave their gods’ bodies and come to Caṇḍikā, each with her god’s form, ornaments and mount (8.12–14): Brahmāṇī in a swan-drawn car with rosary and water-pot (8.15), Māheśvarī on the bull with trident, serpent bracelets and crescent moon (8.16), Kaumārī with spear and peacock (8.17), Vaiṣṇavī on Garuḍa with conch, discus, mace, bow and sword (8.18), Vārāhī in the body of the Sacrificial Boar (8.19), Nārasiṃhī, whose tossing mane scatters the stars (8.20), and Aindrī, thunderbolt in hand, on the king of elephants, thousand-eyed (8.21). Śiva, surrounded by them, asks Caṇḍikā to slay the asuras at once (8.22). From the Goddess’s own body comes forth her Śakti, howling like a hundred jackals (8.23), and sends Śiva himself as her messenger to Śumbha and Niśumbha (8.24–25): let Indra have the three worlds and the gods their offerings, and let the asuras go to Pātāla if they wish to live — or come and feed her jackals (8.26–27). So she is known as Śivadūtī (8.28). The asuras march in indignation to where Kātyāyanī stands (8.29) and rain weapons on her (8.30); she cuts them down as if in play (8.31). Kālī ranges at the front with spear and skull-staff (8.32); Brahmāṇī saps the enemy with water from her pot (8.33); Māheśvarī, Vaiṣṇavī and Kaumārī strike with trident, discus and spear (8.34); Aindrī’s thunderbolt fells them in hundreds (8.35); Vārāhī gores them with snout and tusks (8.36); Nārasiṃhī tears them with her claws and devours them (8.37); Śivadūtī fells them with her laughter and eats them (8.38). The asura soldiers slip away (8.39), and Raktabīja advances (8.40): whenever a drop of his blood falls to the ground, an asura of his own stature springs up (8.41). Aindrī’s thunderbolt draws his blood (8.42), and warriors with his form and strength rise from every drop and fight the Mothers (8.43–45); a second stroke yields thousands (8.46); Vaiṣṇavī’s discus and Aindrī’s mace (8.47) fill the world with his doubles (8.48); Kaumārī, Vārāhī and Māheśvarī strike him (8.49), he strikes back at each Mother with his mace (8.50), and from every wound hundreds more arise (8.51), until the whole world is overrun and the gods are seized with fear (8.52). Caṇḍikā tells Kālī — Cāmuṇḍā — to open her mouth wide (8.53), to catch the blood her weapons draw and the asuras born of it (8.54), and to range the field devouring them, so that, drained of blood, he will perish (8.55–56); then she strikes him with her spear (8.56). Kālī takes his blood in her mouth; his mace-blow on Caṇḍikā (8.57) causes her not the slightest pain, while his blood pours out (8.58). Cāmuṇḍā catches it wherever it flows, eats the asuras born in her mouth and drinks his blood (8.59–60), and the Goddess strikes him down with spear, thunderbolt, arrows, swords and blades; he falls, bloodless (8.60–62). The gods rejoice beyond measure, and the band of Mothers born from them dances, wild with blood (8.62–63).
Forms of the Devī
Caṇḍikā (चण्डिका, 8.8, 8.13, 8.22, 8.53, 8.57), the Goddess at the centre of the battle, also Ambikā (8.9), the Devī (8.11, 8.30, 8.56, 8.60), Kātyāyanī (8.29) and ‘the Unconquered’ (अपराजिता, 8.24); Kālī (8.10, 8.11, 8.32, 8.53, 8.57), called by her name of chapter 7, Cāmuṇḍā (8.53, 8.59, 8.60, 8.61), the mouth that drinks Raktabīja’s blood; the Śaktis of the gods, each in her god’s likeness (8.13–14): Brahmāṇī (8.15, 8.33), Māheśvarī (8.16, 8.34, 8.49), Kaumārī — called अम्बिका गुहरूपिणी, ‘the Mother in the form of Guha’ (8.17) — Vaiṣṇavī (8.18, 8.34, 8.47), Vārāhī (8.19, 8.36, 8.49), Nārasiṃhī (8.20, 8.37) and Aindrī (8.21, 8.35, 8.42, 8.47), together ‘the band of Mothers’ (मातृगण, 8.39, 8.40, 8.63); and Caṇḍikā’s own Śakti, howling like a hundred jackals, named Śivadūtī because she made Śiva her messenger (8.23, 8.28, 8.38). Bhavānī, of the dhyāna, is the form of the liturgy, not of the text.
Significance of the events
This is the third and greatest of the forces sent against the Devī after Dhūmralocana (chapter 6) and Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa (chapter 7): not a general and his army but the whole Daitya host, with Śumbha himself at its head (8.3–7), though he does not meet her until chapter 9. The chapter brings two new things into the war. First, the Mothers: the powers of the gods leave their bodies and fight for Caṇḍikā in the gods’ own likeness (8.13–14). The text here calls them the gods’ śaktis; only later does the Goddess tell Śumbha that all these powers are hers and take them back into herself (10.5–6). Second, an enemy whom wounding only multiplies (8.41): every Mother’s blow fills the world with more Raktabījas (8.43–52), and the battle the Mothers are winning becomes the gods’ fear. The turn is not a stronger weapon but Cāmuṇḍā’s mouth (8.53–61), the devouring power born in chapter 7 (7.9–13), which catches the blood before it reaches the ground. Between the two stands Śivadūtī, the Goddess’s own Śakti, who makes Śiva himself her envoy (8.24–28) and offers the asuras peace on the gods’ terms (8.26–27).
Sanskrit patterns to watch
A narrative chapter of perfects — reduplicated (आदिदेश, निर्जगाम, ययुः, आययौ, जघान, चिच्छेद, चचार, चखाद, सुस्राव, समुत्तस्थुः, ववाह, जगृहे, पपौ, पपात, ननर्त), the unreduplicated पेतुः and नेशुः (8.35, 8.39), the ātmanepada युयुधे beside the epic युयुधुः (8.42, 8.45), and the periphrastic पूरयामास, ताडयामास (8.8, 8.47) — beside imperfects (व्यचरत्, अकरोत्, न्यपतन्, अताडयत्), one without its augment (उपबृंहयत्, 8.9) and one epic thematic form (अहनत्, 8.50). Past participles stand for finite verbs at each arrival (आयाता, प्राप्ता, विनिष्क्रान्ता, 8.15–23). Commands come in three persons: Śumbha’s third-person imperatives to absent troops (निर्यान्तु, निर्गच्छन्तु, 8.4–6), Śiva’s passive imperative (हन्यन्ताम्, 8.22), and the Goddess’s second-person orders (गच्छ, ब्रूहि, 8.24–25; आगच्छत, 8.27; कुरु, प्रतीच्छ, चर, 8.53–55). The chapter is built on correlatives: locative absolutes (8.2), यस्य … यद्रूपम् … तद्वत् (8.14), यतः … ततः (8.28), येन येन … स्म (8.33), यदा … तदा (8.41), यावन्तः … तावन्तः (8.44). The Mothers’ names are vṛddhi derivatives with the feminine -ī (माहेश्वरी, कौमारी, वैष्णवी, वाराही, नारसिंही, ऐन्द्री) or -ānī (ब्रह्माणी). Sentences run across verses at 8.2–3, 8.12–13, 8.59–60, 8.60–61 and 8.61–62.
The teaching (as distinct from the narrative)
Literally, the gods’ powers come out of their bodies to fight beside the Goddess, an asura whose every drop of blood becomes another asura fills the world with his doubles, and Kālī drinks his blood so that he can be killed. As teaching, the chapter shows the one power acting in many forms: each Mother is a god’s power in that god’s shape (8.14), yet Kaumārī is called simply ‘the Mother’ (8.17), and the tradition, following 10.5–6, reads all of them as the Devī’s own. In the Śākta reading Śiva’s errand (8.24–28) shows the god acting at the bidding of Śakti; that is a reading of the tradition, not a statement of the verse, which says only that she sent him and so won her name. Raktabīja is often read today as a picture of desire or habit that grows every time it is fought on its own terms; that reading is modern. What the verses themselves say is plain: striking him harder only multiplied him (8.43–52), and he died when his blood was received before it touched the ground (8.54–61).

Themes

  • The Mothers: the gods’ powers in the gods’ forms
  • Śivadūtī: the Goddess who sends Śiva
  • Raktabīja: an enemy multiplied by every wound
  • Cāmuṇḍā’s mouth: ending the source, not the copies
  • Battle in sound before battle in arms
Recited before the chapter (liturgical, from the Gita Press pāṭha)

These lines belong to the recitation, not to the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa text, and are kept apart from the numbered mantras. They are given here in Sanskrit and IAST only.

Dhyāna

ॐ अरुणां करुणातरङ्गिताक्षीं
धृतपाशाङ्कुशबाणचापहस्ताम्।
अणिमादिभिरावृतां मयूखै-
रहमित्येव विभावये भवानीम्॥

ॐ aruṇāṃ karuṇātaraṅgitākṣīṃ
dhṛtapāśāṅkuśabāṇacāpahastām|
aṇimādibhirāvṛtāṃ mayūkhai-
rahamityeva vibhāvaye bhavānīm||

8.1Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

ॐ ऋषिरुवाच ॥ १ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ऋषिः | उवाच

3IAST

As recitedṛṣiruvāca ||

Separatedṛṣiḥ | uvāca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ऋषिःṛṣiḥthe Ṛṣi, the seer (Medhas)m. nom. sg. of ऋषि (i-stem); subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, kartari prayoga

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    The final s of ऋषिस् becomes ru. Not being at a pause or before an unvoiced sound, it does not become visarga (खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः, 8.3.15, does not apply) and surfaces as r before the vowel: ऋषिरुवाच. After i (not a/ā) the r stays.

C. Morphology

  • उवाच uvāca — Perfect of √वच् with samprasāraṇa in the reduplicated syllable (व → उ): उ-वाच. The standing formula of the speaker labels, as in 1.1.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe Ṛṣi said:

ReadableThe Seer said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas, the seer of the frame story, takes up the narrative again for King Suratha (whom he will address as नृप and भूप in this chapter). Chapter 7 ended with Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa slain and Kālī named Cāmuṇḍā (7.27); chapter 8 tells of the Mothers and Raktabīja. In recitation this mantra carries the bīja ॐ, which belongs to the pāṭha, not to the verse.

8.2Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

चण्डे च निहते दैत्ये मुण्डे च विनिपातिते।
बहुलेषु च सैन्येषु क्षयितेष्वसुरेश्वरः ॥ २ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

चण्डे | च | निहते | दैत्ये | मुण्डे | च | विनिपातितेबहुलेषु | च | सैन्येषु | क्षयितेषु | असुरेश्वरः

3IAST

As recitedcaṇḍe ca nihate daitye muṇḍe ca vinipātite |
bahuleṣu ca sainyeṣu kṣayiteṣvasureśvaraḥ ||

Separatedcaṇḍe | ca | nihate | daitye | muṇḍe | ca | vinipātite
bahuleṣu | ca | sainyeṣu | kṣayiteṣu | asureśvaraḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
चण्डेcaṇḍeCaṇḍam. loc. sg. of चण्ड; locative absolute with निहते
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
निहतेnihatehaving been slainpast passive participle (kta) of नि + √हन् (adādi 2P ‘to strike, kill’), m. loc. sg.; predicate of the locative absolute
दैत्येdaityethe Daityam. loc. sg. of दैत्य (taddhita: ‘descendant of Diti’); in apposition to चण्डे
मुण्डेmuṇḍeMuṇḍam. loc. sg. of मुण्ड; second locative absolute
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
विनिपातितेvinipātitehaving been struck downpast passive participle (kta) of the causative of वि + नि + √पत् (bhvādi 1P ‘to fall’), stem पातय- ‘to fell’; m. loc. sg.
बहुलेषुbahuleṣumany, numerousm./n. loc. pl. of बहुल; qualifies सैन्येषु
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
सैन्येषुsainyeṣuthe troops, the forcesm./n. loc. pl. of सैन्य (taddhita from सेना: ‘belonging to an army’, a soldier or a body of troops); third locative absolute
क्षयितेषुkṣayiteṣuhaving been destroyedpast passive participle (kta) of the causative क्षयय- of √क्षि ‘to destroy’; m./n. loc. pl.; agrees with सैन्येषु
असुरेश्वरःasureśvaraḥthe lord of the asuras (Śumbha)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of आदिदेश in 8.3

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • क्षयितेष्वसुरेश्वरः kṣayiteṣvasureśvaraḥ → क्षयितेषु + असुरेश्वरः
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final u before a dissimilar vowel becomes v: क्षयितेषु + असुर- → क्षयितेष्वसुर-.
  • असुरेश्वरः asureśvaraḥ → असुर + ईश्वरः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ī → e.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरेश्वरः asureśvaraḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: असुर + ईश्वर · Vigraha: असुराणाम् ईश्वरः asurāṇām īśvaraḥ
    Literally “lord of the asuras”; in the verse: Śumbha, named in the next verse

C. Morphology

  • निहते nihate — Locative absolute (sati saptamī): a noun and a participle in the locative state the circumstance — चण्डे निहते ‘Caṇḍa having been slain’. The verse strings three of them (Caṇḍa, Muṇḍa, the troops) before the subject appears.
  • विनिपातिते vinipātite — Participle of a causative: पातय- ‘make fall’ + -इत. The same locative absolute, सिंहे च विनिपातिते, is in Śumbha’s order at 6.24.
  • क्षयितेषु kṣayiteṣu — From the causative of √क्षि (क्षययति ‘destroys’); the same participle described Dhūmralocana’s army at 6.20 (बलं च क्षयितं कृत्स्नम्).

D. Anvaya — prose order

दैत्ये चण्डे निहते, मुण्डे च विनिपातिते, बहुलेषु सैन्येषु च क्षयितेषु, असुरेश्वरः (शुम्भः …)।

daitye caṇḍe nihate, muṇḍe ca vinipātite, bahuleṣu sainyeṣu ca kṣayiteṣu, asureśvaraḥ (śumbhaḥ …) |

Three locative absolutes give the situation; the subject असुरेश्वरः waits for its verb, आदिदेश, in 8.3.

6Translation

LiteralWhen the Daitya Caṇḍa had been slain and Muṇḍa struck down, and the many troops destroyed, the lord of the asuras —

ReadableWhen the demon Caṇḍa had been slain and Muṇḍa struck down, and their many troops destroyed, the lord of the asuras —

The sentence runs on into 8.3, where the verb stands.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningA summary of chapter 7: Caṇḍa was beheaded (7.20), Muṇḍa felled (7.21), and their army was destroyed or fled (7.22). Each word for the deaths is a past participle; the defeat is stated as already complete.

Traditional interpretationThe narrative pattern of the third carita is escalation: one general (Dhūmralocana, ch. 6), then two (Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, ch. 7), now the whole host. Each defeat draws out a greater manifestation of the Goddess.

8.3Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ततः कोपपराधीनचेताः शुम्भः प्रतापवान्।
उद्योगं सर्वसैन्यानां दैत्यानामादिदेश ह ॥ ३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ततः | कोपपराधीनचेताः | शुम्भः | प्रतापवान्उद्योगम् | सर्वसैन्यानाम् | दैत्यानाम् | आदिदेश | ह

3IAST

As recitedtataḥ kopaparādhīnacetāḥ śumbhaḥ pratāpavān |
udyogaṃ sarvasainyānāṃ daityānāmādideśa ha ||

Separatedtataḥ | kopaparādhīnacetāḥ | śumbhaḥ | pratāpavān
udyogam | sarvasainyānām | daityānām | ādideśa | ha

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time, तद् + tas)
कोपपराधीनचेताःkopaparādhīnacetāḥhis mind in the grip of angerm. nom. sg. of the s-stem bahuvrīhi कोपपराधीनचेतस्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies शुम्भः
शुम्भःśumbhaḥŚumbham. nom. sg.; proper name; subject of आदिदेश
प्रतापवान्pratāpavānthe mighty, the majesticm. nom. sg. of प्रतापवत् (प्रताप + matup, ‘possessing might’); qualifies शुम्भः
उद्योगम्udyogamthe mobilization, the setting out to warm. acc. sg. of उद्योग (उद् + √युज्); object of आदिदेश
सर्वसैन्यानाम्sarvasainyānāmof all the troopsm./n. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to दैत्यानाम्
दैत्यानाम्daityānāmof the Daityasm. gen. pl. of दैत्य; depends on उद्योगम्
आदिदेशādideśaorderedआ + √दिश् (tudādi 6P ‘to point out’; with आ ‘to command’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
हhaindeed (a narrative particle, untranslated)indeclinable (expletive particle of narration)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कोपपराधीनचेताः kopaparādhīnacetāḥ → कोप + पर + अधीन + चेताः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    पर + अधीन → पराधीन: a + a merge into ā.
  • उद्योगं सर्वसैन्यानां udyogaṃ sarvasainyānāṃ → उद्योगम् + सर्वसैन्यानाम्
    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; so too सर्वसैन्यानां before द.
  • दैत्यानामादिदेश daityānāmādideśa → दैत्यानाम् + आदिदेश
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Before a vowel the m stays m and is written together with the next word.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • कोपपराधीनचेताः kopaparādhīnacetāḥ — bahuvrīhi (its member पराधीन is itself a tatpuruṣa: परस्य अधीनम्, ‘dependent on another’)
    Members: कोप + पराधीन + चेतस् · Vigraha: कोपस्य पराधीनं चेतः यस्य सः kopasya parādhīnaṃ cetaḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally “one whose mind is dependent on anger”; in the verse: Śumbha, mastered by his rage
    Alternative: The first two members may also be read कोपेन पराधीनम् (‘made dependent by anger’); the sense is the same: anger, not Śumbha, is in control.
  • सर्वसैन्यानाम् sarvasainyānām — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + सैन्य · Vigraha: सर्वाणि च तानि सैन्यानि च, तेषाम् sarvāṇi ca tāni sainyāni ca, teṣām
    Literally “of all the troops”; in the verse: the whole army, not a detachment

C. Morphology

  • आदिदेश ādideśa — Perfect of आ + √दिश्: reduplication दि-देश with guṇa of the root vowel in the singular. With आ it means ‘command’; the object उद्योगम् is the thing commanded.
  • कोपपराधीनचेताः kopaparādhīnacetāḥ — An s-stem bahuvrīhi (-चेतस्) lengthens its vowel in the masculine nominative singular: -चेताः (like सुमनाः).
  • ह ha — The narrative particle ह, common in Purāṇic past tense, adds no lexical meaning and is usually left untranslated.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ततः कोपपराधीनचेताः प्रतापवान् शुम्भः (असुरेश्वरः) सर्वसैन्यानां दैत्यानाम् उद्योगम् आदिदेश ह।

tataḥ kopaparādhīnacetāḥ pratāpavān śumbhaḥ (asureśvaraḥ) sarvasainyānāṃ daityānām udyogam ādideśa ha |

The subject of 8.2 (असुरेश्वरः) is now named: शुम्भः with two qualifiers; the verb आदिदेश governs उद्योगम्, with the double genitive saying whose mobilization.

6Translation

Literalthen Śumbha, the mighty, his mind dependent on anger, ordered the setting out of the Daityas, of all the troops.

Readable— Śumbha, the mighty, his mind wholly mastered by anger, ordered all the Daitya armies to march.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningŚumbha answers the loss of Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa with a total mobilization. The narrator stresses that the order comes from a mind not its own master (पराधीन).

Traditional interpretationThrough the Śumbha cycle the asura king acts from pride and anger (6.3 सक्रोधः, here कोपपराधीनचेताः), never from counsel; the tradition reads the asuras as passions that grow stronger the more they are opposed.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the compound’s own word for Śumbha’s state is पराधीन, ‘dependent on another’. The mighty king is, in that moment, not free; anger has taken the throne.

8.4Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अद्य सर्वबलैर्दैत्याः षडशीतिरुदायुधाः।
कम्बूनां चतुरशीतिर्निर्यान्तु स्वबलैर्वृताः ॥ ४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अद्य | सर्वबलैः | दैत्याः | षडशीतिः | उदायुधाःकम्बूनाम् | चतुरशीतिः | निर्यान्तु | स्वबलैः | वृताः

3IAST

As recitedadya sarvabalairdaityāḥ ṣaḍaśītirudāyudhāḥ |
kambūnāṃ caturaśītirniryāntu svabalairvṛtāḥ ||

Separatedadya | sarvabalaiḥ | daityāḥ | ṣaḍaśītiḥ | udāyudhāḥ
kambūnām | caturaśītiḥ | niryāntu | svabalaiḥ | vṛtāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अद्यadyatoday, nowindeclinable (adverb of time)
सर्वबलैःsarvabalaiḥwith all (their) forcesn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument of accompaniment with निर्यान्तु
दैत्याःdaityāḥthe Daityasm. nom. pl. of दैत्य; subject of the first clause
षडशीतिःṣaḍaśītiḥeighty-six (clans)f. nom. sg. of the numeral षडशीति; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to दैत्याः
उदायुधाःudāyudhāḥwith weapons raisedm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies दैत्याः
कम्बूनाम्kambūnāmof the Kambus (an asura clan)m. gen. pl. of कम्बु (u-stem); depends on चतुरशीतिः
चतुरशीतिःcaturaśītiḥeighty-four (clans)f. nom. sg. of the numeral चतुरशीति; compound (see Samāsa); subject of the second clause
निर्यान्तुniryāntulet them go forth, march outनिस् + √या (adādi 2P ‘to go’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
स्वबलैःsvabalaiḥby their own forcesn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent with वृताः
वृताःvṛtāḥsurroundedpast passive participle (kta) of √वृ (svādi 5 ‘to cover, surround’), m. nom. pl.

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सर्वबलैर्दैत्याः sarvabalairdaityāḥ → सर्वबलैः + दैत्याः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After a vowel other than a/ā, visarga (as ru) surfaces as r before a voiced consonant; so too स्वबलैर्वृताः and चतुरशीतिर्निर्यान्तु.
  • षडशीतिरुदायुधाः ṣaḍaśītirudāyudhāḥ → षडशीतिः + उदायुधाः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    -iḥ before a vowel → -ir.
  • षडशीति ṣaḍaśīti → षट् + अशीति
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    षट् ‘six’ voices its final ṭ to ḍ before the vowel of अशीति.
  • निर्यान्तु niryāntu → निस् + यान्तु
    prefix sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    The s of the prefix निस् becomes r before the voiced y.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वबलैः sarvabalaiḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + बल · Vigraha: सर्वाणि च तानि बलानि च, तैः sarvāṇi ca tāni balāni ca, taiḥ
    Literally “with all forces”; in the verse: with their entire strength
  • षडशीतिः ṣaḍaśītiḥ — karmadhāraya (numeral compound of the śākapārthiva type, the middle word अधिका understood)
    Members: षष् + अशीति · Vigraha: षडधिका अशीतिः ṣaḍadhikā aśītiḥ
    Literally “eighty increased by six”; in the verse: eighty-six
  • उदायुधाः udāyudhāḥ — bahuvrīhi (prādi: the first member उद् stands for उद्यत)
    Members: उद् + आयुध · Vigraha: उद्यतानि आयुधानि येषां ते udyatāni āyudhāni yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those whose weapons are raised”; in the verse: armed and ready to strike
    Alternative: Some take Udāyudha as the name of a clan, parallel to the Kambus of the next line: ‘the eighty-six Udāyudhas’. The adjective reading is chosen because it needs no name the text nowhere else uses.
  • चतुरशीतिः caturaśītiḥ — karmadhāraya (numeral compound, as षडशीतिः)
    Members: चतुर् + अशीति · Vigraha: चतुरधिका अशीतिः caturadhikā aśītiḥ
    Literally “eighty increased by four”; in the verse: eighty-four
  • स्वबलैः svabalaiḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: स्व + बल · Vigraha: स्वानि बलानि, तैः svāni balāni, taiḥ
    Literally “with own forces”; in the verse: each clan with its own troops

C. Morphology

  • षडशीतिः ṣaḍaśītiḥ — Numerals from twenty upward are feminine singular nouns (विंशतिः, अशीतिः); they stand in apposition to what is counted or take it in the genitive. Both constructions are here: दैत्याः षडशीतिः, कम्बूनां चतुरशीतिः.
  • निर्यान्तु niryāntu — Third-person imperative (loṭ) of √या: a command to absent subjects, ‘let them march’.
  • वृताः vṛtāḥ — Past participle of √वृ ‘to cover’, used for an escort: ‘surrounded by’ with the instrumental.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अद्य उदायुधाः षडशीतिः दैत्याः सर्वबलैः (निर्यान्तु); कम्बूनां चतुरशीतिः स्वबलैः वृताः निर्यान्तु।

adya udāyudhāḥ ṣaḍaśītiḥ daityāḥ sarvabalaiḥ (niryāntu); kambūnāṃ caturaśītiḥ svabalaiḥ vṛtāḥ niryāntu |

Śumbha’s order in direct speech: two subjects, each a numeral, share the one imperative निर्यान्तु.

6Translation

LiteralToday let the Daityas, eighty-six, with weapons raised, (go forth) with all their forces; let eighty-four of the Kambus go forth, surrounded by their own forces.

Readable“Today let the eighty-six Daitya clans march out with all their forces, weapons raised, and the eighty-four clans of the Kambus, each surrounded by its own troops.

What is counted is not named; ‘clans’ is supplied from कुलानि in 8.5. The plural वृताः is construed with the eighty-four by sense.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningŚumbha’s command begins (8.4–6). He names asura clans by number; the list conveys the size of the host rather than an order of battle.

Traditional interpretationThe Kambus and the clans of 8.5–6 belong to the Purāṇic lore of the asura races; the text names them once and does not describe them again.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: उदायुधाः may be an epithet (‘with weapons raised’) or a clan name (‘the Udāyudhas’), and the noun counted by षडशीतिः and चतुरशीतिः is not stated (clans, or chiefs). The translation follows the epithet reading and supplies ‘clans’ from 8.5.

8.5Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

कोटिवीर्याणि पञ्चाशदसुराणां कुलानि वै।
शतं कुलानि धौम्राणां निर्गच्छन्तु ममाज्ञया ॥ ५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

कोटिवीर्याणि | पञ्चाशत् | असुराणाम् | कुलानि | वैशतम् | कुलानि | धौम्राणाम् | निर्गच्छन्तु | मम | आज्ञया

3IAST

As recitedkoṭivīryāṇi pañcāśadasurāṇāṃ kulāni vai |
śataṃ kulāni dhaumrāṇāṃ nirgacchantu mamājñayā ||

Separatedkoṭivīryāṇi | pañcāśat | asurāṇām | kulāni | vai
śatam | kulāni | dhaumrāṇām | nirgacchantu | mama | ājñayā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
कोटिवीर्याणिkoṭivīryāṇiof crore-fold valour; the Koṭivīrya (clans)n. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies कुलानि
पञ्चाशत्pañcāśatfiftyf. nom. sg. of the numeral पञ्चाशत् (t-stem); in apposition to कुलानि
असुराणाम्asurāṇāmof the asurasm. gen. pl. of असुर; depends on कुलानि
कुलानिkulānifamilies, clansn. nom. pl. of कुल; subject of निर्गच्छन्तु
वैvaiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
शतम्śatama hundredn. nom. sg. of the numeral शत; in apposition to the second कुलानि
कुलानिkulānifamilies, clansn. nom. pl. of कुल; second subject of निर्गच्छन्तु
धौम्राणाम्dhaumrāṇāmof the Dhaumrasm. gen. pl. of धौम्र (taddhita from धूम्र: ‘descendants of Dhūmra’); depends on कुलानि
निर्गच्छन्तुnirgacchantulet them go outनिस् + √गम् (bhvādi 1P, present stem गच्छ-), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
ममmamamyfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; depends on आज्ञया
आज्ञयाājñayāby the commandf. instr. sg. of आज्ञा (आ + √ज्ञा)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • पञ्चाशदसुराणां pañcāśadasurāṇāṃ → पञ्चाशत् + असुराणाम्
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before a vowel; the final m of असुराणाम् becomes anusvāra before क (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • ममाज्ञया mamājñayā → मम + आज्ञया
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • शतं कुलानि śataṃ kulāni → शतम् + कुलानि
    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • निर्गच्छन्तु nirgacchantu → निस् + गच्छन्तु
    prefix sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    The s of निस् becomes r before the voiced g, as in निर्यान्तु (8.4).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • कोटिवीर्याणि koṭivīryāṇi — bahuvrīhi
    Members: कोटि + वीर्य · Vigraha: कोटिः वीर्याणि येषां तानि koṭiḥ vīryāṇi yeṣāṃ tāni
    Literally “(clans) whose valour is a crore”; in the verse: the Koṭivīryas, clans of immense might
    Alternative: Taken by many as the name of a clan (Koṭivīrya) rather than a description; as a bahuvrīhi agreeing with कुलानि it serves as both: ‘the fifty Koṭivīrya clans’.

C. Morphology

  • पञ्चाशत् pañcāśat — A feminine t-stem numeral, used in the singular whatever the number counted: पञ्चाशत् … कुलानि ‘fifty clans’.
  • धौम्राणाम् dhaumrāṇām — Vṛddhi taddhita (धूम्र → धौम्र) forming a patronymic or clan name: ‘those of Dhūmra’s line’.
  • निर्गच्छन्तु nirgacchantu — Imperative third plural of निस् + √गम्, with the present stem गच्छ-; parallel to निर्यान्तु (8.4, 8.6).

D. Anvaya — prose order

असुराणां कोटिवीर्याणि पञ्चाशत् कुलानि, धौम्राणां शतं कुलानि (च) मम आज्ञया वै निर्गच्छन्तु।

asurāṇāṃ koṭivīryāṇi pañcāśat kulāni, dhaumrāṇāṃ śataṃ kulāni (ca) mama ājñayā vai nirgacchantu |

Two numbered subjects (पञ्चाशत् कुलानि, शतं कुलानि) share the imperative निर्गच्छन्तु; मम आज्ञया gives the authority.

6Translation

LiteralThe fifty families of the asuras, of crore-fold valour, indeed — a hundred families of the Dhaumras — let them go out at my command.

ReadableLet the fifty asura clans of the Koṭivīryas, and the hundred clans of the Dhaumras, march out at my command.

‘and’ is supplied between the two subjects.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe muster continues: fifty Koṭivīrya clans and a hundred Dhaumra clans. The phrase मम आज्ञया makes the order personal; it returns in the next verse.

Traditional interpretationDhaumra is formed from Dhūmra, ‘smoke-coloured’, the word that named Dhūmralocana (‘Smoke-eye’), slain by a mere हुंकार of Ambikā (6.13). Whether the text means his kin is not stated; the name may simply mark another asura line.

8.6Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

कालका दौर्हृदा मौर्याः कालकेयास्तथासुराः।
युद्धाय सज्जा निर्यान्तु आज्ञया त्वरिता मम ॥ ६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

कालकाः | दौर्हृदाः | मौर्याः | कालकेयाः | तथा | असुराःयुद्धाय | सज्जाः | निर्यान्तु | आज्ञया | त्वरिताः | मम

3IAST

As recitedkālakā daurhṛdā mauryāḥ kālakeyāstathāsurāḥ |
yuddhāya sajjā niryāntu ājñayā tvaritā mama ||

Separatedkālakāḥ | daurhṛdāḥ | mauryāḥ | kālakeyāḥ | tathā | asurāḥ
yuddhāya | sajjāḥ | niryāntu | ājñayā | tvaritāḥ | mama

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
कालकाःkālakāḥthe Kālakas (an asura clan)m. nom. pl. of कालक; subject of निर्यान्तु
दौर्हृदाःdaurhṛdāḥthe Daurhṛdas (an asura clan; the name means ‘of the ill-hearted’)m. nom. pl. of दौर्हृद (vṛddhi taddhita from दुर्हृद् ‘ill-hearted, hostile’)
मौर्याःmauryāḥthe Mauryas (an asura clan)m. nom. pl. of मौर्य
कालकेयाःkālakeyāḥthe Kālakeyas (an asura clan, ‘sons of Kālakā’)m. nom. pl. of कालकेय (taddhita -एय, a metronymic from कालका)
तथाtathālikewise, andindeclinable (adverb of manner, used as a conjunction)
असुराःasurāḥthe asurasm. nom. pl. of असुर; sums up the list
युद्धायyuddhāyafor battlen. dat. sg. of युद्ध (√युध् + kta); dative of purpose
सज्जाःsajjāḥready, equippedm. nom. pl. of the adjective सज्ज; predicate with निर्यान्तु
निर्यान्तुniryāntulet them go forthनिस् + √या (adādi 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
आज्ञयाājñayāat the commandf. instr. sg. of आज्ञा
त्वरिताःtvaritāḥin haste, swiftlypast participle (kta) of √त्वर् (bhvādi 1Ā ‘to hurry’), m. nom. pl.; used adverbially
ममmamamyfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; depends on आज्ञया

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कालका दौर्हृदा मौर्याः kālakā daurhṛdā mauryāḥ → कालकाः + दौर्हृदाः + मौर्याः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced consonant: the visarga (as ru) becomes y after ā, and that y is then dropped before a consonant, leaving -ā. So too युद्धाय सज्जा निर्यान्तु and त्वरिता मम.
  • कालकेयास्तथासुराः kālakeyāstathāsurāḥ → कालकेयाः + तथा + असुराः
    visarga sandhi and vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Visarga before t becomes s (कालकेयास्तथा); ā + a → ā (तथासुराः).

C. Morphology

  • कालकेयाः kālakeyāḥ — The suffix -एय forms metronymics (descent through the mother): कालकेय ‘son of Kālakā’, as कौन्तेय ‘son of Kuntī’.
  • त्वरिताः tvaritāḥ — A past participle in agreement with the subject but adverbial in sense: ‘let them go forth, hastened’ = ‘let them go at once’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

कालकाः दौर्हृदाः मौर्याः कालकेयाः तथा (अन्ये) असुराः युद्धाय सज्जाः मम आज्ञया त्वरिताः निर्यान्तु।

kālakāḥ daurhṛdāḥ mauryāḥ kālakeyāḥ tathā (anye) asurāḥ yuddhāya sajjāḥ mama ājñayā tvaritāḥ niryāntu |

A list of clans sums up in असुराः; the imperative निर्यान्तु has two predicate adjectives (सज्जाः, त्वरिताः) and the instrumental of authority, मम आज्ञया.

Left unjoined: निर्यान्तु | आज्ञया — Hiatus at the pāda boundary: निर्यान्तु closes the third pāda and आज्ञया opens the fourth. Joining them (निर्यान्त्वाज्ञया) would erase the pāda break; the verse, like much epic and Purāṇic verse, leaves vowels unjoined across a pāda boundary (a metrical licence). The Gita Press text prints them apart.

6Translation

LiteralThe Kālakas, the Daurhṛdas, the Mauryas, the Kālakeyas, and likewise the asuras — ready for battle let them go forth, hastening at my command.

ReadableLet the Kālakas, the Daurhṛdas, the Mauryas and the Kālakeyas — every asura — march out at once at my command, armed for battle.”

तथासुराः is rendered as summing up the list (‘every asura’); it may also mean ‘and the other asuras’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe muster ends with four more asura clans and the repeated मम आज्ञया. The haste (त्वरिताः) matches Śumbha’s anger in 8.3.

Traditional interpretationThe Kālakeyas are known from the Mahābhārata: they are the asuras who hide in the ocean until Agastya drinks it dry, and Arjuna later fights them with the Paulomas. The Devī Māhātmya draws its enemies from the same old lineages of the asuras.

8.7Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

इत्याज्ञाप्यासुरपतिः शुम्भो भैरवशासनः।
निर्जगाम महासैन्यसहस्रैर्बहुभिर्वृतः ॥ ७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

इति | आज्ञाप्य | असुरपतिः | शुम्भः | भैरवशासनःनिर्जगाम | महासैन्यसहस्रैः | बहुभिः | वृतः

3IAST

As recitedityājñāpyāsurapatiḥ śumbho bhairavaśāsanaḥ |
nirjagāma mahāsainyasahasrairbahubhirvṛtaḥ ||

Separatediti | ājñāpya | asurapatiḥ | śumbhaḥ | bhairavaśāsanaḥ
nirjagāma | mahāsainyasahasraiḥ | bahubhiḥ | vṛtaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
इतिitithus (closing the speech)indeclinable (particle of quotation)
आज्ञाप्यājñāpyahaving commandedabsolutive (lyap) of the causative of आ + √ज्ञा (kryādi 9P ‘to know’; causative आज्ञापय- ‘to command’)
असुरपतिःasurapatiḥthe lord of the asurasm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to शुम्भः
शुम्भःśumbhaḥŚumbham. nom. sg.; subject of निर्जगाम
भैरवशासनःbhairavaśāsanaḥwhose commands are terriblem. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies शुम्भः
निर्जगामnirjagāmawent out, set forthनिस् + √गम् (bhvādi 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
महासैन्यसहस्रैःmahāsainyasahasraiḥby thousands of great troopsn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent with वृतः
बहुभिःbahubhiḥmanyn. instr. pl. of बहु (u-stem); qualifies महासैन्यसहस्रैः
वृतःvṛtaḥsurroundedpast passive participle (kta) of √वृ (svādi 5), m. nom. sg.; qualifies शुम्भः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • इत्याज्ञाप्यासुरपतिः ityājñāpyāsurapatiḥ → इति + आज्ञाप्य + असुरपतिः
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ and savarṇa-dīrgha) · इको यणचि (6.1.77); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    i before ā becomes y (इत्याज्ञाप्य); a + a → ā (आज्ञाप्यासुर-).
  • शुम्भो भैरवशासनः śumbho bhairavaśāsanaḥ → शुम्भः + भैरवशासनः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.
  • सहस्रैर्बहुभिर्वृतः sahasrairbahubhirvṛtaḥ → सहस्रैः + बहुभिः + वृतः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After ai and i the visarga surfaces as r before the voiced b and v.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरपतिः asurapatiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: असुर + पति · Vigraha: असुराणां पतिः asurāṇāṃ patiḥ
    Literally “lord of the asuras”; in the verse: Śumbha as king
  • भैरवशासनः bhairavaśāsanaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: भैरव + शासन · Vigraha: भैरवं शासनं यस्य सः bhairavaṃ śāsanaṃ yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose command is terrible”; in the verse: a ruler whose orders are feared
  • महासैन्यसहस्रैः mahāsainyasahasraiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (its first member महासैन्य is a karmadhāraya: महत् च तत् सैन्यं च)
    Members: महासैन्य + सहस्र · Vigraha: महासैन्यानां सहस्राणि, तैः mahāsainyānāṃ sahasrāṇi, taiḥ
    Literally “by thousands of great troops”; in the verse: by an immense host

C. Morphology

  • आज्ञाप्य ājñāpya — Absolutive of a causative with a prefix: the stem आज्ञापय- drops -अय before the suffix -य (lyap, used instead of -त्वा when a prefix is present).
  • निर्जगाम nirjagāma — Perfect of √गम्: the reduplicated syllable has j for g (ज-गाम), with vṛddhi of the root vowel in the third singular.

D. Anvaya — prose order

इति आज्ञाप्य भैरवशासनः असुरपतिः शुम्भः बहुभिः महासैन्यसहस्रैः वृतः निर्जगाम।

iti ājñāpya bhairavaśāsanaḥ asurapatiḥ śumbhaḥ bahubhiḥ mahāsainyasahasraiḥ vṛtaḥ nirjagāma |

An absolutive clause (इति आज्ञाप्य), the subject with two qualifiers, and the main verb निर्जगाम with the participle phrase बहुभिः … वृतः.

6Translation

LiteralHaving commanded thus, Śumbha, the lord of the asuras, of terrible command, went out, surrounded by many thousands of great troops.

ReadableHaving given these orders, Śumbha, lord of the asuras, whose commands were dreaded, set out, surrounded by many thousands of mighty troops.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningŚumbha now leaves his city with the host. Until now he had sent others: the messenger Sugrīva (5.102), Dhūmralocana (6.3), Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa.

Traditional interpretationŚumbha does not meet the Goddess in person until chapter 9 (9.8, 9.17); here he is the commander who sets the whole host in motion, and the narrative turns at once to the Goddess’s answer.

8.8Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

आयान्तं चण्डिका दृष्ट्वा तत्सैन्यमतिभीषणम्।
ज्यास्वनैः पूरयामास धरणीगगनान्तरम् ॥ ८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

आयान्तम् | चण्डिका | दृष्ट्वा | तत्सैन्यम् | अतिभीषणम्ज्यास्वनैः | पूरयामास | धरणीगगनान्तरम्

3IAST

As recitedāyāntaṃ caṇḍikā dṛṣṭvā tatsainyamatibhīṣaṇam |
jyāsvanaiḥ pūrayāmāsa dharaṇīgaganāntaram ||

Separatedāyāntam | caṇḍikā | dṛṣṭvā | tatsainyam | atibhīṣaṇam
jyāsvanaiḥ | pūrayāmāsa | dharaṇīgaganāntaram

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
आयान्तम्āyāntamcoming, approaching (him, Śumbha)present participle (śatṛ) of आ + √या (adādi 2P), m. acc. sg.; object of दृष्ट्वा
चण्डिकाcaṇḍikāCaṇḍikā, the Fierce Onef. nom. sg. of चण्डिका; subject
दृष्ट्वाdṛṣṭvāhaving seenabsolutive (ktvā) of √दृश् (bhvādi 1P ‘to see’)
तत्सैन्यम्tatsainyamhis armyn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); second object of दृष्ट्वा
अतिभीषणम्atibhīṣaṇamexceedingly terriblen. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तत्सैन्यम्
ज्यास्वनैःjyāsvanaiḥwith the twangs of (her) bowstringm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument of पूरयामास
पूरयामासpūrayāmāsafilledcausative of √पॄ (‘to fill’), stem पूरय-; periphrastic perfect (liṭ with आम्), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
धरणीगगनान्तरम्dharaṇīgaganāntaramthe space between earth and skyn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of पूरयामास

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • आयान्तं चण्डिका āyāntaṃ caṇḍikā → आयान्तम् + चण्डिका
    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • तत्सैन्यमतिभीषणम् tatsainyamatibhīṣaṇam → तत्सैन्यम् + अतिभीषणम्
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    The m is kept and written together with the vowel.
  • धरणीगगनान्तरम् dharaṇīgaganāntaram → धरणीगगन + अन्तरम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • तत्सैन्यम् tatsainyam — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: तद् + सैन्य · Vigraha: तस्य सैन्यम् tasya sainyam
    Literally “the army of him”; in the verse: Śumbha’s host
    Alternative: Read as two words, तत् सैन्यम् ‘that army’. But आयान्तम् is masculine and cannot agree with the neuter सैन्यम्; it must mean Śumbha himself, and तत्सैन्यम् ‘his army’ then completes the picture.
  • अतिभीषणम् atibhīṣaṇam — prādi tatpuruṣa (karmadhāraya with the prefix अति ‘exceedingly’)
    Members: अति + भीषण · Vigraha: अतिशयेन भीषणम् atiśayena bhīṣaṇam
    Literally “exceedingly terrible”; in the verse: dreadful to see
  • ज्यास्वनैः jyāsvanaiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: ज्या + स्वन · Vigraha: ज्यायाः स्वनाः, तैः jyāyāḥ svanāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “with the sounds of the bowstring”; in the verse: with the twanging of her bow
  • धरणीगगनान्तरम् dharaṇīgaganāntaram — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa whose first member is an itaretara dvandva
    Members: धरणीगगन + अन्तर · Vigraha: धरणी च गगनं च धरणीगगने; तयोः अन्तरम् dharaṇī ca gaganaṃ ca dharaṇīgagane; tayoḥ antaram
    Literally “the interval of earth and sky”; in the verse: all the space between ground and heaven

C. Morphology

  • आयान्तम् āyāntam — Present participle आयत् (आ + √या + śatṛ), masculine accusative singular with the strong stem आयान्त्-. Its gender shows that the one ‘coming’ is Śumbha, not the (neuter) army.
  • पूरयामास pūrayāmāsa — Periphrastic perfect: causative and other derived stems form the perfect with -आम् and a perfect of √अस् (पूरय् + आम् + आस).

D. Anvaya — prose order

चण्डिका आयान्तं (शुम्भम्) अतिभीषणं तत्सैन्यं (च) दृष्ट्वा ज्यास्वनैः धरणीगगनान्तरं पूरयामास।

caṇḍikā āyāntaṃ (śumbham) atibhīṣaṇaṃ tatsainyaṃ (ca) dṛṣṭvā jyāsvanaiḥ dharaṇīgaganāntaraṃ pūrayāmāsa |

An absolutive clause with two objects (आयान्तम्, तत्सैन्यम्), then the main verb पूरयामास with its instrument and object.

6Translation

LiteralCaṇḍikā, having seen (him) approaching, and his exceedingly terrible army, filled the space between earth and sky with the twangs of her bowstring.

ReadableSeeing Śumbha approach with his dreadful army, Caṇḍikā filled the space between earth and sky with the twanging of her bowstring.

‘Śumbha’ is supplied from the masculine participle आयान्तम्; see Samāsa.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess answers the muster with sound before any weapon is loosed. The bowstring’s twang filling heaven and earth recalls her first battle, where the same sound shook the underworlds (2.38, धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेन).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāCaṇḍikā is also a name in the Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa), name 755, चण्डिका. In the Devī Māhātmya it is the commonest name of the Goddess in battle.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: आयान्तम् is masculine; most renderings take it loosely with the army (‘seeing that army approaching’). The analysis reads it strictly, of Śumbha, who set out in 8.7.

8.9Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ततः सिंहो महानादमतीव कृतवान् नृप।
घण्टास्वनेन तन्नादमम्बिका चोपबृंहयत् ॥ ९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ततः | सिंहः | महानादम् | अतीव | कृतवान् | नृपघण्टास्वनेन | तन्नादम् | अम्बिका | च | उपबृंहयत्

3IAST

As recitedtataḥ siṃho mahānādamatīva kṛtavān nṛpa |
ghaṇṭāsvanena tannādamambikā copabṛṃhayat ||

Separatedtataḥ | siṃhaḥ | mahānādam | atīva | kṛtavān | nṛpa
ghaṇṭāsvanena | tannādam | ambikā | ca | upabṛṃhayat

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
सिंहःsiṃhaḥthe lion (the Goddess’s mount)m. nom. sg. of सिंह; subject of कृतवान्
महानादम्mahānādama great roarm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of कृतवान्
अतीवatīvaexceedingly, mightilyindeclinable (adverb, अति + इव)
कृतवान्kṛtavānmade, let outpast active participle (ktavatu) of √कृ (tanādi 8 ‘to do’), m. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
नृपnṛpaO king (Suratha)m. voc. sg. of नृप
घण्टास्वनेनghaṇṭāsvanenawith the sound of her bellm. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument of उपबृंहयत्
तन्नादम्tannādamthat roar of his (the lion’s)m. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of उपबृंहयत्
अम्बिकाambikāAmbikā, the Motherf. nom. sg. of अम्बिका; subject of उपबृंहयत्
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
उपबृंहयत्upabṛṃhayatswelled, augmentedउप + causative of √बृह् (‘to grow’; stem बृंहय- ‘to make great’), laṅ (imperfect) without the augment, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सिंहो महानादमतीव siṃho mahānādamatīva → सिंहः + महानादम् + अतीव
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced m becomes -o; the m of महानादम् is kept before the vowel of अतीव.
  • अतीव atīva → अति + इव
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    i + i → ī; the fused adverb is written as one word.
  • तन्नादम् tannādam → तद् + नादम्
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (nasalisation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    A stop before a nasal may become the nasal of its class: d before n → n.
  • चोपबृंहयत् copabṛṃhayat → च + उपबृंहयत्
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महानादम् mahānādam — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + नाद · Vigraha: महान् चासौ नादः च, तम् mahān cāsau nādaḥ ca, tam
    Literally “a great roar”; in the verse: a mighty roar
  • घण्टास्वनेन ghaṇṭāsvanena — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: घण्टा + स्वन · Vigraha: घण्टायाः स्वनः, तेन ghaṇṭāyāḥ svanaḥ, tena
    Literally “with the sound of the bell”; in the verse: with the clanging of her bell
  • तन्नादम् tannādam — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: तद् + नाद · Vigraha: तस्य नादः, तम् tasya nādaḥ, tam
    Literally “the roar of it”; in the verse: the lion’s roar
    Alternative: Not two words: a separate तत् is neuter and could not agree with the masculine नादम्.

C. Morphology

  • कृतवान् kṛtavān — The past active participle (-तवत्) is often used for a finite past: सिंहः … कृतवान् ‘the lion made’.
  • उपबृंहयत् upabṛṃhayat — The regular imperfect would be उपाबृंहयत् (the augment अ stands between prefix and verb). The augmentless form is an epic licence (or an injunctive with past sense); the meaning is unaffected.
  • नृप nṛpa — Vocative: Medhas addresses King Suratha, his listener.

D. Anvaya — prose order

नृप, ततः सिंहः अतीव महानादं कृतवान्; अम्बिका च घण्टास्वनेन तन्नादम् उपबृंहयत्।

nṛpa, tataḥ siṃhaḥ atīva mahānādaṃ kṛtavān; ambikā ca ghaṇṭāsvanena tannādam upabṛṃhayat |

Two clauses joined by च: the lion’s roar, and Ambikā’s bell amplifying it.

6Translation

LiteralThen the lion made an exceedingly great roar, O king, and Ambikā swelled that roar of his with the sound of her bell.

ReadableThen, O king, the lion gave a mighty roar, and Ambikā swelled that roar with the clanging of her bell.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe second and third sounds join the bowstring of 8.8: the lion’s roar and the bell. Ambikā and Caṇḍikā are the same Goddess under two names.

Traditional interpretationThe bell was Indra’s gift, taken from Airāvata (2.22); in the first battle its sound bewildered the asuras (2.56), and the gods pray to be guarded by it (4.24; 11.27). Its sound is itself a weapon that weakens the enemy.

8.10Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानां नादापूरितदिङ्मुखा।
निनादैर्भीषणैः काली जिग्ये विस्तारितानना ॥ १० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानाम् | नादापूरितदिङ्मुखानिनादैः | भीषणैः | काली | जिग्ये | विस्तारितानना

3IAST

As reciteddhanurjyāsiṃhaghaṇṭānāṃ nādāpūritadiṅmukhā |
ninādairbhīṣaṇaiḥ kālī jigye vistāritānanā ||

Separateddhanurjyāsiṃhaghaṇṭānām | nādāpūritadiṅmukhā
ninādaiḥ | bhīṣaṇaiḥ | kālī | jigye | vistāritānanā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानाम्dhanurjyāsiṃhaghaṇṭānāmof the bowstring, the lion and the bellf. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on the member नाद- of the next word
नादापूरितदिङ्मुखाnādāpūritadiṅmukhāthe quarters (around her) filled with the soundf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies काली
निनादैःninādaiḥwith roarsm. instr. pl. of निनाद; instrument of जिग्ये
भीषणैःbhīṣaṇaiḥterriblem. instr. pl. of भीषण; qualifies निनादैः
कालीkālīKālīf. nom. sg. of काली; subject
जिग्येjigyeprevailed, outdid (them)√जि (bhvādi 1P ‘to conquer’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, with an ātmanepada ending (the regular active is जिगाय)
विस्तारिताननाvistāritānanāher mouth stretched widef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies काली

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • धनुर्ज्या dhanurjyā → धनुस् + ज्या
    visarga sandhi inside a compound · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    The s of धनुस् becomes r before the voiced j.
  • नादापूरित nādāpūrita → नाद + आपूरित
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā; so too विस्तारित + आनना → विस्तारितानना.
  • दिङ्मुखा diṅmukhā → दिश् + मुखा
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (nasalisation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    दिश् first takes the form दिक् at the end of a member; k before the nasal m becomes ṅ.
  • निनादैर्भीषणैः ninādairbhīṣaṇaiḥ → निनादैः + भीषणैः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After ai the visarga surfaces as r before the voiced bh.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानाम् dhanurjyāsiṃhaghaṇṭānām — itaretara dvandva (its first member a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa)
    Members: धनुर्ज्या + सिंह + घण्टा · Vigraha: धनुर्ज्या च सिंहः च घण्टा च, तेषाम् (धनुर्ज्या = धनुषः ज्या) dhanurjyā ca siṃhaḥ ca ghaṇṭā ca, teṣām (dhanurjyā = dhanuṣaḥ jyā)
    Literally “of bowstring, lion and bell”; in the verse: the three sounds of 8.8–9
  • नादापूरितदिङ्मुखा nādāpūritadiṅmukhā — bahuvrīhi (containing a tṛtīyā relation नादेन आपूरित and a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa दिङ्मुख)
    Members: नाद + आपूरित + दिङ्मुख · Vigraha: नादेन आपूरितानि दिङ्मुखानि यस्याः (परितः) सा (दिङ्मुखानि = दिशां मुखानि) nādena āpūritāni diṅmukhāni yasyāḥ (paritaḥ) sā (diṅmukhāni = diśāṃ mukhāni)
    Literally “she around whom the faces of the quarters are filled with sound”; in the verse: Kālī amid the din that fills every direction
    Alternative: As in 7.8, where the same epithet describes Kālī, it can mean ‘she by whom the quarters are filled with sound’ (नादेन आपूरितानि दिङ्मुखानि यया सा). Here the sound is said to be that of the bowstring, lion and bell, which are not Kālī’s, so the looser ‘around whom’ is chosen. The genitive धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानाम् is construed with the member नाद- (an epic licence).
  • विस्तारितानना vistāritānanā — bahuvrīhi
    Members: विस्तारित + आनन · Vigraha: विस्तारितम् आननं यया सा vistāritam ānanaṃ yayā sā
    Literally “she by whom the mouth is stretched wide”; in the verse: with gaping mouth (cf. अतिविस्तारवदना, 7.8)

C. Morphology

  • जिग्ये jigye — Perfect of √जि with an ātmanepada ending. √जि is parasmaipada (जिगाय); जिग्ये could formally be a passive perfect (‘was conquered’), but with काली as subject and the instrumental निनादैः the sense is active: ‘she prevailed (over those sounds) with her roars’.
  • विस्तारितानना vistāritānanā — विस्तारित is the past participle of the causative of वि + √स्तृ ‘to spread’: ‘made wide’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानां नादापूरितदिङ्मुखा विस्तारितानना काली भीषणैः निनादैः जिग्ये।

dhanurjyāsiṃhaghaṇṭānāṃ nādāpūritadiṅmukhā vistāritānanā kālī bhīṣaṇaiḥ ninādaiḥ jigye |

The subject काली carries two bahuvrīhis; the verb जिग्ये has the instrument भीषणैः निनादैः. What she outdid — the sounds just named — is understood.

6Translation

LiteralKālī — the faces of the quarters about her filled with the sound of bowstring, lion and bell — her mouth stretched wide, prevailed with terrible roars.

ReadableWith the twang of the bow, the lion’s roar and the clang of the bell filling every quarter, Kālī, her mouth gaping wide, outdid them all with her terrible roars.

The object of ‘outdid’ (the three sounds) is supplied; जिग्ये is taken in its active sense.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe fourth sound, Kālī’s roar, tops the other three. Both epithets repeat her portrait at her birth (7.8: नादापूरितदिङ्मुखा, अतिविस्तारवदना).

Traditional interpretationBefore the armies close, the battle is fought in sound. The scene returns at Śumbha’s own onset (9.19–22): conch, bowstring, bell and lion fill the quarters, then Kālī strikes earth and sky and ‘the earlier sounds were drowned out’ (प्राक्स्वनास्ते तिरोहिताः, 9.22). At 9.26 √जि is again used of one sound overcoming another (जितवान्), which supports the reading of जिग्ये here.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: The construction is loose: the genitive धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानाम् hangs on the member नाद- of a compound, and जिग्ये is an ātmanepada perfect of a parasmaipada root. The reading ‘Kālī outdid those sounds with her roars’ is supported by the parallel at 9.22 and 9.26; ‘Kālī, who filled the quarters with the sound …, prevailed with her roars’ is also possible.

8.11Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तं निनादमुपश्रुत्य दैत्यसैन्यैश्चतुर्दिशम्।
देवी सिंहस्तथा काली सरोषैः परिवारिताः ॥ ११ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तम् | निनादम् | उपश्रुत्य | दैत्यसैन्यैः | चतुर्दिशम्देवी | सिंहः | तथा | काली | सरोषैः | परिवारिताः

3IAST

As recitedtaṃ ninādamupaśrutya daityasainyaiścaturdiśam |
devī siṃhastathā kālī saroṣaiḥ parivāritāḥ ||

Separatedtam | ninādam | upaśrutya | daityasainyaiḥ | caturdiśam
devī | siṃhaḥ | tathā | kālī | saroṣaiḥ | parivāritāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तम्tamthatpronoun तद्, m. acc. sg.; qualifies निनादम्
निनादम्ninādamroar, dinm. acc. sg. of निनाद; object of उपश्रुत्य
उपश्रुत्यupaśrutyahaving heardabsolutive (lyap) of उप + √श्रु (svādi 5P ‘to hear’)
दैत्यसैन्यैःdaityasainyaiḥby the Daitya armiesn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of the passive परिवारिताः
चतुर्दिशम्caturdiśamon all four sidescompound (see Samāsa), n. acc. sg. used adverbially
देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी; subject
सिंहःsiṃhaḥthe lionm. nom. sg. of सिंह; subject
तथाtathāand alsoindeclinable (used as a conjunction)
कालीkālīKālīf. nom. sg. of काली; subject
सरोषैःsaroṣaiḥenraged, furiousn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies दैत्यसैन्यैः
परिवारिताःparivāritāḥwere surroundedpast passive participle (kta) of the causative of परि + √वृ (‘to surround’), f. nom. pl.; predicate (supply आसन्)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तं निनादमुपश्रुत्य taṃ ninādamupaśrutya → तम् + निनादम् + उपश्रुत्य
    anusvāra; final m before a vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    तम् before n becomes तं; निनादम् before the vowel of उपश्रुत्य keeps its m.
  • दैत्यसैन्यैश्चतुर्दिशम् daityasainyaiścaturdiśam → दैत्यसैन्यैः + चतुर्दिशम्
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś.
  • सिंहस्तथा siṃhastathā → सिंहः + तथा
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • दैत्यसैन्यैः daityasainyaiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: दैत्य + सैन्य · Vigraha: दैत्यानां सैन्यानि, तैः daityānāṃ sainyāni, taiḥ
    Literally “by the armies of the Daityas”; in the verse: by Śumbha’s host
  • चतुर्दिशम् caturdiśam — dvigu (samāhāra), used adverbially
    Members: चतुर् + दिश् · Vigraha: चतसृणां दिशां समाहारः catasṛṇāṃ diśāṃ samāhāraḥ
    Literally “the group of four quarters”; in the verse: on every side
    Alternative: Also treated as an avyayībhāva, ‘in the four quarters’; the adverbial sense is the same.
  • सरोषैः saroṣaiḥ — bahuvrīhi (saha-bahuvrīhi, स for सह)
    Members: स + रोष · Vigraha: रोषेण सह वर्तन्ते इति सरोषाणि, तैः roṣeṇa saha vartante iti saroṣāṇi, taiḥ
    Literally “with anger”; in the verse: furious

C. Morphology

  • परिवारिताः parivāritāḥ — The participle is feminine plural although the lion is masculine. Strict grammar lets the masculine prevail in a mixed group; the feminine here follows the two goddesses who head the list (an epic licence of agreement).
  • उपश्रुत्य upaśrutya — In a passive sentence the absolutive goes with the agent in the instrumental: it is the Daitya armies who heard the roar and then surrounded the three.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तं निनादम् उपश्रुत्य सरोषैः दैत्यसैन्यैः देवी सिंहः तथा काली चतुर्दिशं परिवारिताः (आसन्)।

taṃ ninādam upaśrutya saroṣaiḥ daityasainyaiḥ devī siṃhaḥ tathā kālī caturdiśaṃ parivāritāḥ (āsan) |

A passive sentence: the subjects (देवी, सिंहः, काली) with the participle परिवारिताः; the agent दैत्यसैन्यैः also does the hearing (उपश्रुत्य).

6Translation

LiteralHaving heard that roar, by the enraged Daitya armies on four sides the Goddess, the lion and also Kālī were surrounded.

ReadableHearing that roar, the furious Daitya armies closed in on every side around the Goddess, the lion and Kālī.

The passive is turned into an active sentence.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe din draws the whole enemy host in, and the three — the Goddess, her lion and Kālī — are encircled. The situation is set for the help that arrives in the next verse.

Traditional interpretationThe three on the Goddess’s side are the Goddess, her mount and Kālī, the power that sprang from her own brow (7.6). The Mothers who now join them are a further multiplication of the one power facing the many.

8.12Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

एतस्मिन्नन्तरे भूप विनाशाय सुरद्विषाम्।
भवायामरसिंहानामतिवीर्यबलान्विताः ॥ १२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एतस्मिन् | अन्तरे | भूप | विनाशाय | सुरद्विषाम्भवाय | अमरसिंहानाम् | अतिवीर्यबलान्विताः

3IAST

As recitedetasminnantare bhūpa vināśāya suradviṣām |
bhavāyāmarasiṃhānāmativīryabalānvitāḥ ||

Separatedetasmin | antare | bhūpa | vināśāya | suradviṣām
bhavāya | amarasiṃhānām | ativīryabalānvitāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
एतस्मिन्etasminin thispronoun एतद्, n. loc. sg.; qualifies अन्तरे
अन्तरेantareinterval, momentn. loc. sg. of अन्तर; locative of time: ‘at this juncture’
भूपbhūpaO king (Suratha)m. voc. sg. of भूप (‘protector of the earth’)
विनाशायvināśāyafor the destructionm. dat. sg. of विनाश; dative of purpose
सुरद्विषाम्suradviṣāmof the enemies of the godsm. gen. pl. of the root-noun compound सुरद्विष्; compound (see Samāsa); depends on विनाशाय
भवायbhavāyafor the well-being, for the prosperitym. dat. sg. of भव; dative of purpose
अमरसिंहानाम्amarasiṃhānāmof the lion-like immortals, of the foremost godsm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on भवाय
अतिवीर्यबलान्विताःativīryabalānvitāḥendowed with surpassing valour and strengthf. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies शक्तयः in 8.13

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • एतस्मिन्नन्तरे etasminnantare → एतस्मिन् + अन्तरे
    consonant sandhi (doubling of n) · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32)
    A final n after a short vowel is doubled before a vowel: -इन् + अ- → -इन्न-.
  • भवायामरसिंहानामतिवीर्य bhavāyāmarasiṃhānāmativīrya → भवाय + अमरसिंहानाम् + अतिवीर्य-
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); final m before a vowel · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā (भवायामर-); the m of अमरसिंहानाम् is kept before the vowel.
  • बलान्विताः balānvitāḥ → बल + अन्विताः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सुरद्विषाम् suradviṣām — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: सुर + द्विष् · Vigraha: सुरान् द्विषन्ति इति सुरद्विषः, तेषाम् surān dviṣanti iti suradviṣaḥ, teṣām
    Literally “of those who hate the gods”; in the verse: of the asuras
  • अमरसिंहानाम् amarasiṃhānām — karmadhāraya (upamita: the standard of comparison stands second, as in पुरुषव्याघ्र)
    Members: अमर + सिंह · Vigraha: अमराः सिंहाः इव, तेषाम् amarāḥ siṃhāḥ iva, teṣām
    Literally “of the immortals who are like lions”; in the verse: of the foremost gods
    Alternative: Or ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, अमराणां सिंहाः ‘the lions among the immortals’, i.e. the chief gods. Both mean the leading gods, whose powers come forth in 8.13.
  • अतिवीर्यबलान्विताः ativīryabalānvitāḥ — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa whose first member is a dvandva qualified by the prefix अति
    Members: अति + वीर्य + बल + अन्वित · Vigraha: अतिशयितं वीर्यं च बलं च अतिवीर्यबले; ताभ्याम् अन्विताः atiśayitaṃ vīryaṃ ca balaṃ ca ativīryabale; tābhyām anvitāḥ
    Literally “joined with exceeding valour and strength”; in the verse: of surpassing might

C. Morphology

  • सुरद्विषाम् suradviṣām — द्विष् is a root used as a noun at the end of a compound (kvip): consonant stem, genitive plural -आम्.
  • अतिवीर्यबलान्विताः ativīryabalānvitāḥ — Feminine plural without its noun: it anticipates शक्तयः in the next verse. The sentence runs from 8.12 into 8.13.

D. Anvaya — prose order

भूप, एतस्मिन् अन्तरे सुरद्विषां विनाशाय अमरसिंहानां भवाय (च) अतिवीर्यबलान्विताः (शक्तयः …)

bhūpa, etasmin antare suradviṣāṃ vināśāya amarasiṃhānāṃ bhavāya (ca) ativīryabalānvitāḥ (śaktayaḥ …)

Time, vocative, two datives of purpose, and an adjective waiting for its noun (शक्तयः) and verb (ययुः) in 8.13.

6Translation

LiteralAt this juncture, O king, for the destruction of the enemies of the gods (and) for the well-being of the lion-like immortals, endowed with surpassing valour and strength —

ReadableAt that very moment, O king, to destroy the enemies of the gods and for the good of the foremost of the immortals, (powers) endowed with surpassing valour and might —

The noun the adjective qualifies (‘powers’) is supplied from 8.13.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe purpose is stated before the actors appear: the śaktis come to destroy the asuras and to secure the gods. The vocative भूप reminds us that Medhas is telling this to Suratha.

Traditional interpretationThe paired datives — destruction of the hostile, welfare of the good — follow the familiar formula of divine descent, as in the Bhagavadgītā (4.8: परित्राणाय साधूनां विनाशाय च दुष्कृताम्).

8.13Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ब्रह्मेशगुहविष्णूनां तथेन्द्रस्य च शक्तयः।
शरीरेभ्यो विनिष्क्रम्य तद्रूपैश्चण्डिकां ययुः ॥ १३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

3IAST

As recitedbrahmeśaguhaviṣṇūnāṃ tathendrasya ca śaktayaḥ |
śarīrebhyo viniṣkramya tadrūpaiścaṇḍikāṃ yayuḥ ||

śarīrebhyaḥ | viniṣkramya | tadrūpaiḥ | caṇḍikām | yayuḥ
